<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the quaternionic-division component of Zilber–Pink for curves in $`\mathcal A_2`$ over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$. A Hodge-generic algebraic curve contains only finitely many points whose full geometric rational endomorphism algebra is an indefinite quaternion division algebra over ℚ. No boundary or reduction hypothesis is required.

- **claim-main-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $C\subset\mathcal A_{2,\overline{\mathbb Q}}$ be a reduced irreducible closed Hodge-generic algebraic curve. Then $$\Sigma_{\mathrm{QM}}(C)= \bigl\{s\in C(\overline{\mathbb Q}): \mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_s)\text{ is an indefinite quaternion division algebra over }\mathbb Q \bigr\}$$ is finite.
  - Explanation: On a Hodge-generic curve in the Siegel threefold, only finitely many points have full geometric rational endomorphism algebra equal to an indefinite quaternion division algebra, with no boundary or reduction hypothesis.
- **claim-fixed-covers** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-covers)): Let $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ be a reduced irreducible closed curve over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$. There is a finite surjective map $\pi:S\to C$ from a smooth geometrically connected curve over a number field, together with a principally polarized abelian scheme $f:\mathcal A\to S$ whose coarse moduli map is $\pi$. One may obtain $S$ by normalization and a fixed full symplectic level $N\geq3$.* *If infinitely many points of $C$ have the endomorphism algebra in [thm:main](#thm:main), then infinitely many points of $S$ have that property. Deleting finitely many points of $S$, or making a further fixed finite surjective cover and selecting a dominating connected component, preserves this assertion. Once all fixed data are defined over $K$, degrees and ample logarithmic heights on the smooth completions satisfy $$\label{eq:fixed-cover-bounds} [K(\pi(t)):K]\leq[K(t):K]\leq D[K(\pi(t)):K],\qquad c^{-1}h_C(\pi(t))-c\leq h_S(t)\leq c h_C(\pi(t))+c,$$ with fixed constants $D,c$. The same statements hold for any further fixed cover. In particular, a degree-dependent polynomial height bound obtained on one selected lift of every target descends to the original targets.
  - Explanation: A curve can be replaced by a smooth connected fine-level cover carrying a universal abelian scheme. Infinitude of the quaternionic locus and polynomial height bounds transfer between cover and curve with controlled degree and height factors.
- **claim-qm-plane** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-qm-plane)): The local system $\mathbb V$ has rank five, a nondegenerate form of real signature $(3,2)$, and Hodge numbers $(1,3,1)$ in types $(1,-1),(0,0),(-1,1)$. It is the primitive part of $R^2f_*\mathbb Q(1)$. Conjugation of operators gives a surjective morphism of algebraic groups $$\label{eq:gsp-to-so} \rho:\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(\mathbb H,\psi)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathbb V,q), \qquad g:T\longmapsto gTg^{-1}, \qquad \ker\rho=\mathbb G_m.$$ Its restriction to $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4$ is the spin covering, with kernel $\{\pm\mathrm{id}\}$.* *At a point $t$ whose full geometric endomorphism algebra is an indefinite quaternion division algebra, the trace-free Rosati-symmetric endomorphisms form a rational two-plane $E_t\subset\mathbb V_t$ of Hodge vectors. The restriction of $q$ to $E_t$ is positive definite. This plane is spanned by two actual integral trace-free symmetric endomorphisms. If $\Lambda_t=\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z,t}$, then $L_t=E_t\cap\Lambda_t$ is saturated in $\Lambda_t$; a selected generating pair need not generate this saturated lattice.
  - Explanation: The trace-free self-adjoint operators form a quadratic local system with a spin-type map from the symplectic similitude group. At a quaternionic point the trace-free Rosati-symmetric endomorphisms give a positive definite rational plane of Hodge vectors, saturated in the integral lattice.
- **claim-monodromy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-monodromy)): For the family over any fixed smooth finite cover of a Hodge-generic curve in $\mathcal A_2$, the connected algebraic monodromy on $\mathbb H$ is $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4$, and that on $\mathbb V$ is $\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(V,q)$. The same holds after pullback by a dominant morphism from an irreducible complex algebraic variety, on a smooth open of a resolution.* *Let $S_1,S_2$ be two such curves, and let an irreducible complex algebraic variety $W$ map dominantly to both. On the sum of the two pulled-back weight-one systems, either the connected monodromy is $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4\times\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4$, or the image of $W$ in the product of the two moduli curves is contained in a dominating component of a polarized isogeny correspondence. In the latter case the corresponding local period relation is given by a fixed element of $\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}_4(\mathbb Q)$ with positive multiplier.
  - Explanation: Connected monodromy is the full symplectic group on the weight-one system and the full orthogonal group on the quadratic system. For two curves, independent monodromy fails only along a polarized isogeny correspondence.
- **claim-finite-correspondences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-correspondences)): Fix finitely many closed irreducible Hodge-generic curves in $\mathcal A_2(\mathbb C)$. For every ordered pair of these curves there are finitely many rational positive projective symplectic similitudes carrying a lift of the first onto a lift of the second, modulo integral changes of lifts. Consequently all dominating polarized isogeny correspondences between this list of curves are contained in a finite union of closed curves in their pairwise products. There is a fixed finite set of positive integers $m$ such that every pair on this union is joined by a polarized isogeny of one of these multipliers, of degree $m^2$. Refining the fixed level does not enlarge the required set of underlying multipliers.
  - Explanation: Between finitely many Hodge-generic curves only finitely many rational similitudes up to integral change of lift exist. Hence the dominating isogeny correspondences lie in a finite union of curves with a fixed set of multipliers.
- **claim-common-relation-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-interpolation)): Fix the analytic data just described, a fixed affine ambient space with coordinates $(a,U)$, and an algebraic ambient locus $X$ in that space. Let $h,d\geq2$, let $\Psi(h)=1+(\log h)^c$ for a fixed $c\geq1$, and let $z_*=(a_*,U_*)\in X(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ with $a_*\ne0$. Suppose that the following bounds hold, with fixed polynomials $Q_i$ having nonnegative coefficients.* 1. *The ambient locus has equations of degree at most $Q_0(d)$ and affine height at most $Q_0(d)\Psi(h)$, and $\mathrm h\!\left(z_*\right)\leq Q_0(d)h$.* 2. *There is a finite weighted selection $\mathcal V$ of places or embeddings for which $\left\lvert a_*\right\rvert_v<1$ and $$\label{eq:interpolation-smallness} S_{\rm small}:=\sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v \log\frac1{\left\lvert a_*\right\rvert_v} \geq\frac{h}{Q_1(d)},\qquad \sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v\log\max(1,\left\lVert U_*\right\rVert_v) \leq Q_2(d)\Psi(h).$$ If the second argument occurs, $\left\lvert R_*\right\rvert_v=1$ for these local data.* 3. *A common list of fixed length $s$ of allowed expressions $$E_\nu(a,U)=\sum_{j\geq0}a^j e_{\nu j}(U),\qquad 1\leq\nu\leq s,$$ vanishes at $z_*$ at every selected local datum. Its coefficients satisfy $$\deg e_{\nu j}\leq C(1+j),\qquad \mathrm h\!\left((e_{\nu j})_{\nu,\,0\leq j\leq l}\right) \leq Q_3(l,d)\Psi(h).$$ There are $b_v\geq0$ with $\sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v b_v\leq Q_4(d)\Psi(h)$ such that the convergent tails at the target satisfy, for every $l\geq0$, $$\label{eq:interpolation-tail} \left\|\sum_{j\geq l}a_*^j e_{\nu j}(U_*)\right\|_v \leq \left\lvert a_*\right\rvert_v^l\exp((1+l)b_v).$$* 4. *If $W\subseteq X$ is an irreducible algebraic variety containing $z_*$ on which $a$ is nonconstant, then on every normalization branch above the generic point of every component of $W\cap V(a)$ the expressions $E_1,\ldots,E_s$ are not all identically zero. The expressions use their specified ordinary chart germs.* *Then $h\leq C_1d^{C_2}$, with constants depending only on the fixed data and the polynomials in the hypotheses.
  - Explanation: Common analytic relations with controlled coefficient heights, small local parameter and nonidentity on every algebraic branch of the descent force a polynomial bound of the height in the complexity parameter.
- **claim-zero-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-zero-estimate)): For the fixed analytic data of [thm:interpolation](#thm:interpolation) there is a polynomial $M(D)$ with the following property. Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of degree at most $D$ on which $a$ is nonconstant. On a branch above the generic point of a component of $V_W(a)$, every allowed expression $E$ which is not identically zero satisfies $$\label{eq:divisorial-zero-estimate} \operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak p}E <M(D)\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak p}a.$$ The bound is independent of the heights of the coefficients of $W$ and of the expression.
  - Explanation: At a branch above a component of the zero locus of the parameter, a nonzero allowed expression vanishes to an order bounded by a polynomial in the degree of the variety times the order of the parameter, independently of coefficient heights.
- **claim-graph-fiber** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-fiber)): Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of dimension $k\geq1$ with coordinate $a$ nonconstant, let $f$ be regular on $W$, and let $m\ge1$ be an integer. Suppose $$\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak p}f <m\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak p}a$$ on every normalization branch over the generic components of $V_W(a)$. Let $W'$ be the affine graph closure of $q=f/a^m$ over $W\setminus V(a)$. For every regular function $g$ on $W$, $$\label{eq:graph-fiber-dimension} \dim V\bigl(I(W'),a,q+g\bigr)\leq k-2.$$ The set is empty when $k=1$; the same convention applies if $V_W(a)$ is empty.
  - Explanation: If the order of the function is below a multiple of the order of the parameter on every branch, the graph closure meets the special fibre and the extra linear condition in codimension at least two.
- **claim-layer-interpolation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-interpolation)): Let $W'\subset\mathbb A^M$ be irreducible of dimension $k\geq1$, with coordinates including $a,U,q$. Let $\mathfrak b=(G_1,\ldots,G_t)$ have controlled generators and $V(\mathfrak b)=W'$. Suppose $$\dim V(\mathfrak b,a,F_0)\leq k-2, \qquad F_0=q+g_0(U),$$ and put $F_n=F_0+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a^j g_j(U)$, where $m\ge1$ is an integer and $\deg g_j\leq C(m+j+1)$. Here $M,C$ are fixed and all other complexities, including $m$, are polynomially bounded in $d$; the coefficient heights through index $n$ are bounded by $Q(n,d)\Psi(h)$.* *For any prescribed positive integer $r$ polynomially bounded in $d$, there are positive integers $L$ and $n=rL$, both polynomially bounded in $d$, and a polynomial $P$ such that $$\label{eq:layer-membership} \deg P\leq L,\quad P\notin I(W'),\quad P=\sum_{\lambda=1}^t A_\lambda G_\lambda+A F_n+a^n B.$$ The degrees of all the coefficients in this representation are polynomial in $d$, and their joint affine height, including that of $P$, is at most $Q'(d)\Psi(h)$. The polynomial $Q'$ may depend on the prescribed polynomial bound for $r$.
  - Explanation: Counting standard monomials layer by layer yields a polynomial of controlled degree, not in the ideal of the graph, that lies in the ideal generated by the defining equations, the truncated relation and a high power of the parameter.
- **claim-potential-good** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-potential-good)): An abelian surface whose geometric endomorphism algebra is a quaternion division algebra over $\mathbb Q$ has potentially good reduction at every finite place.
  - Explanation: A positive toric rank would make the character space a module over the division algebra, of dimension too large, so the toric rank vanishes and good reduction is reached after a finite extension.
- **claim-small-vectors** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-small-vectors)): There are constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. For every target $t$, an extension $L/K(t)$ of degree at most $C$ defines all geometric endomorphisms of $A_t$. There are two independent endomorphisms $u_1,u_2\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_L(A_t)$ which are symmetric and trace free, span $E_t$, and satisfy $$\label{eq:rosati-size} q_{\lambda_t}(u_j)\le C(dh)^c\qquad(j=1,2).$$ The corresponding labeled vectors are denoted $P=(P_1,P_2)$ in de Rham coordinates. Conjugating a labeled target always means conjugating these endomorphisms as well. This pair need not generate the saturated integral cohomology lattice of $E_t$.
  - Explanation: Each target has, over an extension of bounded degree, two independent symmetric trace-free endomorphisms spanning the plane with Rosati norms polynomially bounded in the degree and height.
- **claim-dr-size** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-dr-size)): Choose the vectors of [prop:small-vectors](#prop:small-vectors). Their affine coordinates in the fixed frame satisfy $$\label{eq:dr-global} \mathrm h\!\left(z(t),a,P\right)\le C(h+\log d).$$ There are center neighborhoods at every place, with a single integral description outside a fixed finite set of rational primes, such that for any selected weighted set $\mathcal V$ on the actual center branches, $$\label{eq:dr-local} \sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v \log\max(1,\left\lVert z(t),P\right\rVert_v) \le C(1+\log h+\log d).$$ These statements hold for the finitely many conjugate fixed charts. They also hold after a bounded denominator and a polarized isogeny of any one fixed multiplier are applied to the vectors.
  - Explanation: In a fixed frame the coordinates of the small vectors have global height linear in the height and logarithmic in the degree, and on center disks the local sizes are only logarithmic.
- **claim-good-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-good-comparison)): At a finite place, suppose a point $t$ is on a sufficiently small actual center branch. After coefficient extension the two polarized abelian schemes have a common special fiber $\overline A$. More precisely, over a finite local extension $F$ with residue field $k$, put $F_0=W(k)[1/p]$ and $D=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\overline A/W(k))[1/p]$. There are rational comparison isomorphisms $$c_u:H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(A_u/F)\longrightarrow D\otimes_{F_0}F, \qquad u=t_0,t,$$ whose transition $c_{t_0}^{-1}c_t$ is horizontal transport. They are compatible with morphisms of either lifted fiber and their specializations, including morphisms which do not extend over the base curve. Ramification of the coefficient field is allowed.* *In particular, if $\beta_i$ and $\alpha_j$ are the specializations of the centered and target endomorphisms, then $$\label{eq:cross-trace} \left\langle B_i,Y(a)P_j\right\rangle=\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\beta_i\alpha_j)\in\mathbb Q \qquad(1\le i,j\le2).$$ The cross pairings belong to $\tfrac14\mathbb Z$, and their ordinary absolute values are at most $C(dh)^c$. At archimedean places the same assertions hold with integral Betti transport in place of specialization.
  - Explanation: Near the center at a finite place the two fibres share a special fibre, and horizontal transport is realised by crystalline-de Rham comparison. Cross pairings of centered and target endomorphisms are then rational, lie in a fixed lattice and are polynomially bounded.
- **claim-proximity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-proximity)): Use the fixed center disks of [prop:dr-size](#prop:dr-size), [prop:good-comparison](#prop:good-comparison), and at every remaining prime impose <a href="#eq:factorial-disk" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:factorial-disk">[eq:factorial-disk]</a>. Let $\mathcal V_0(t)$ be all weighted embeddings at which $t$ belongs to these actual branches. Then $$\label{eq:proximity-sum} \sum_{v\in\mathcal V_0(t)}w_v\log(1/\|a\|_v) \ge c h-C\bigl(1+\log^2(dh)\bigr).$$ In particular the sum is at least $c h/2$ if $h\ge Q_0(d)$ for a fixed polynomial $Q_0$.
  - Explanation: The weighted smallness of the parameter along actual center branches grows linearly in the height up to a polylogarithmic loss caused by the factorial cutoff.
- **claim-common-relations** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-common-relations)): Suppose $h\ge Q_0(d)$, increasing $Q_0$ if necessary. The proximity embeddings can be partitioned into at most $Q_1(d)$ groups, and one group $\mathcal V$ has $$\label{eq:group-smallness} S_{\mathrm{small}}= \sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v\log(1/\|a\|_v) \ge h/Q_2(d).$$ On each group there is one fixed list of analytic equations, evaluated at one algebraic tuple $(a,U)$, of one of the following forms: $$\begin{aligned} \left\langle B,Y(a)P\right\rangle&=T, &&\text{with }T\in\mathrm{M}_2(\mathbb Q), \label{eq:constant-relations}\\ \left\langle B,Y(a)P\right\rangle&=\left\langle B',Y'(aR)P'\right\rangle, &&R=b/a,\quad b=x'(t'), \label{eq:paired-relations} \end{aligned}$$ together with the actual branch equations $$\label{eq:chart-relations} z-z(a)=0, \qquad z'-z'(aR)=0 \quad\text{when a second chart is present}.$$ The primed data are one simultaneous field conjugate of the complete labeled unprimed data. In <a href="#eq:paired-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:paired-relations">[eq:paired-relations]</a> all places are finite and $\|R\|_v=1$ on the group. The Frobenius of the common reduction transports each of the two labeled pairs to its primed pair.* *The tuple has degree at most $Q_3(d)$ over $K$, and $$\label{eq:common-size} \mathrm h\!\left(a,U\right)\le Q_3(d)h, \qquad \sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v\log\max(1,\left\lVert U\right\rVert_v) \le Q_3(d)(1+\log h).$$ Writing the common list as $E(a,U)=\sum_{j\ge0}a^j e_j(U)$, it has fixed length and $$\label{eq:coefficient-size} \begin{aligned} \deg e_j&\le C(1+j) &&(j\ge0),\\ \mathrm h\!\left(\text{affine coefficient tuple of }(e_0,\ldots,e_l)\right) &\le Q_4(l,d)(1+\log h) &&(l\ge0). \end{aligned}$$ There are $b_v\ge0$ with $$\begin{aligned} \left\\|\sum_{j\ge l}a^j e_j(U)\right\\|_v &\le \|a\|_v^l\exp\bigl((1+l)b_v\bigr) &&(l\ge0),\label{eq:arithmetic-tail}\\ \sum_{v\in\mathcal V}w_v b_v &\le Q_5(d)(1+\log^2 h). \label{eq:tail-sum} \end{aligned}$$ The series are formed from fixed ordinary-point algebraic connection systems in $a$ and $aR$, and fixed algebraic chart series, by polynomial operations of fixed degree.
  - Explanation: Frobenius eliminates the rational trace matrix. A polynomial number of groups of places is formed, and one group carries a common list of analytic equations with controlled heights and tail bounds.
- **claim-finite-field-incidence** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-field-incidence)): Let $k$ be a finite field of odd characteristic, let $(V,q)$ be a nondegenerate quadratic space of dimension five, let $A\subset V$ be a nondegenerate $2$-plane, and let $T\subset V$ be any $2$-plane. There is an element $$u\in\operatorname{im}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(V)(k)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(V)(k)\bigr)$$ such that $A\cap uT=0$ and $A+uT$ is a degenerate $4$-plane, with radical of dimension one. The conclusion includes restricted quadratic forms on $T$ of ranks zero, one, and two.
  - Explanation: Over a finite field of odd characteristic, an element lifting to the spin group moves any plane to meet a given nondegenerate plane in a degenerate four-space with one-dimensional radical.
- **claim-marking** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-marking)): There is a fixed odd prime $\ell$ and a fixed geometrically connected finite étale cover $S\to S_*$ parameterizing symplectic markings at level $\ell$, with a fixed lift $t_0$ of $t_{0,*}$, such that every quaternionic-division point of $S_*$ outside a finite set has a lift $t\in S$ satisfying the following condition. In the common quadratic space supplied by the markings, $$\label{eq:marked-incidence} \dim\bigl(\overline L_{t_0}+\overline L_t\bigr)=4, \qquad q\|_{\overline L_{t_0}+\overline L_t}\text{ is degenerate}.$$ The prime $\ell$ is prime to every $m\in\mathcal M$. At every prime $p\ne\ell$ of good reduction for these fixed models where $t$ and $t_0$ have the same marked reduction, the corresponding two saturated $\mathbb Z_\ell$-lattices have sum $M$ satisfying $$\label{eq:primitive-four-lattice} M=\Lambda_\ell\cap(M\otimes_{\mathbb Z_\ell}\mathbb Q_\ell), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M=4, \qquad M/\ell M\text{ is degenerate}.$$ These assertions are preserved by simultaneous field conjugation.
  - Explanation: One auxiliary prime and a connected cover of markings guarantee that, for all but finitely many quaternionic targets, the centered and target saturated planes span a degenerate four-space modulo that prime, with a saturated integral sum.
- **claim-fixed-isogeny-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-isogeny-reduction)): Fix finitely many pairs of principally polarized abelian surfaces over a number field and finitely many positive integers $m$. Outside a fixed finite set of rational primes, every polarized isogeny of multiplier $m$ between the geometric reductions of a fixed pair is the specialization of a characteristic-zero polarized isogeny of that multiplier between the pair. The set of these characteristic-zero maps is finite. The assertion concerns the maps themselves, and holds for supersingular reductions as well.
  - Explanation: Away from finitely many primes, every polarized isogeny of fixed multiplier between reductions of a fixed pair is the specialization of one of finitely many characteristic-zero isogenies, also at supersingular reductions.
- **claim-nonidentity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nonidentity)): For a selected marked target as in [prop:marking](#prop:marking), each applicable list above, formed from a nonempty selected group of places, satisfies the all-branch nonidentity hypothesis of [thm:interpolation](#thm:interpolation). More precisely, let $W$ be any irreducible algebraic subvariety of the indicated affine ambient conditions which contains the target and on which $a$ is nonconstant. On every branch over the generic point of every component of $W\cap\{a=0\}$, at least one equation in the applicable list, including the chart equations, is not an identity.
  - Explanation: Monodromy rules out identities for constant and paired relations. In the exceptional isogeny case a common sign argument and the degenerate marking exclude both signs, so some equation is never an identity.
- **claim-height-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-height)): Fix a reduced irreducible closed Hodge-generic curve $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, and let $C^\nu$ be its normalization. For a fixed nonnegative ample logarithmic height $h$ on the smooth projective completion of $C^\nu$ and a fixed field of definition $K$, there are constants $C_1,C_2>0$ such that every point $s\in C^\nu(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ whose coarse image has full geometric rational endomorphism algebra an indefinite quaternion division algebra over $\mathbb Q$ satisfies $$\max\{2,h(s)\}\le C_1\max\{2,[K(s):K]\}^{C_2}.$$ The same assertion holds on the fixed fine-level curves used above, and for a fixed projective height of the coarse points. The constants do not depend on the quaternion algebra, its discriminant, or the target endomorphism order.
  - Explanation: The height of every quaternionic point is polynomially bounded in its degree over the field of definition, with constants independent of the quaternion algebra, its discriminant and the endomorphism order.
- **claim-flat-coordinate-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-flat-coordinate-bound)): There is a finite collection of simply connected complex charts covering $S(\mathbb C)$, each equipped with an integral flat basis of $\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z}$, whose period maps are definable in $\mathbb R_{\mathrm{an},\exp}$. There are constants $c,M>0$ such that, for every QM point $t\in S(\overline{\mathbb Q})$, the ordered pair of integral vectors supplied by [prop:small-vectors](#prop:small-vectors) has flat coordinate tuple $y\in\mathbb Z^{10}$ satisfying $$\label{eq:flat-coordinate-height} H_{\mathrm{rat}}(y)\le c\bigl(d(t)h(t)\bigr)^M$$ in each of these charts containing $t$. The same bound holds for their conjugate vectors at every conjugate of $t$ over $K$.
  - Explanation: Finitely many definable charts carry integral flat bases in which the integral coordinates of the two Hodge vectors have rational height polynomially bounded in degree and height, also at conjugates.
- **claim-counting-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-counting-finiteness)): For fixed $A,\kappa>0$, the set of QM points $t\in S(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ satisfying $$\label{eq:counting-height-hypothesis} h(t)\le A d(t)^\kappa$$ is finite.
  - Explanation: Quaternionic points whose height is polynomially bounded in their degree are finite in number: a large Galois orbit would give a definable path forcing the period image into a variety of too small dimension, contradicting Zariski density.

The same holds after pullback by a dominant morphism from an irreducible complex algebraic variety, on a smooth open of a resolution.*\n\n*Let $S_1,S_2$ be two such curves, and let an irreducible complex algebraic variety $W$ map dominantly to both. On the sum of the two pulled-back weight-one systems, either the connected monodromy is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_4\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_4$, or the image of $W$ in the product of the two moduli curves is contained in a dominating component of a polarized isogeny correspondence. In the latter case the corresponding local period relation is given by a fixed element of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GSp}}_4(\\mathbb Q)$ with positive multiplier.",
      "explanation": "Connected monodromy is the full symplectic group on the weight-one system and the full orthogonal group on the quadratic system. For two curves, independent monodromy fails only along a polarized isogeny correspondence.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-monodromy",
          "exact_quote": "For the family over any fixed smooth finite cover of a Hodge-generic curve in $\\mathcal A_2$",
          "source_id": "PM-016.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-monodromy",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-monodromy",
          "exact_quote": "any fixed smooth finite cover",
          "source_id": "PM-016.3"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-monodromy"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-correspondences",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many closed irreducible Hodge-generic curves in $\\mathcal A_2(\\mathbb C)$. For every ordered pair of these curves there are finitely many rational positive projective symplectic similitudes carrying a lift of the first onto a lift of the second, modulo integral changes of lifts. Consequently all dominating polarized isogeny correspondences between this list of curves are contained in a finite union of closed curves in their pairwise products. There is a fixed finite set of positive integers $m$ such that every pair on this union is joined by a polarized isogeny of one of these multipliers, of degree $m^2$. Refining the fixed level does not enlarge the required set of underlying multipliers.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-016.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the analytic data just described, a fixed affine ambient space with coordinates $(a,U)$, and an algebraic ambient locus $X$ in that space. Let $h,d\\geq2$, let $\\Psi(h)=1+(\\log h)^c$ for a fixed $c\\geq1$, and let $z_*=(a_*,U_*)\\in X(\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$ with $a_*\\ne0$. Suppose that the following bounds hold, with fixed polynomials $Q_i$ having nonnegative coefficients.*\n\n1.  *The ambient locus has equations of degree at most $Q_0(d)$ and affine height at most $Q_0(d)\\Psi(h)$, and $\\mathrm h\\!\\left(z_*\\right)\\leq Q_0(d)h$.*\n\n\n2.  *There is a finite weighted selection $\\mathcal V$ of places or embeddings for which $\\left\\lvert a_*\\right\\rvert_v<1$ and $$\\label{eq:interpolation-smallness}\n     S_{\\rm small}:=\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v\n                   \\log\\frac1{\\left\\lvert a_*\\right\\rvert_v}\n     \\geq\\frac{h}{Q_1(d)},\\qquad\n     \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v\\log\\max(1,\\left\\lVert U_*\\right\\rVert_v)\n     \\leq Q_2(d)\\Psi(h).$$ If the second argument occurs, $\\left\\lvert R_*\\right\\rvert_v=1$ for these local data.*\n\n3.  *A common list of fixed length $s$ of allowed expressions $$E_\\nu(a,U)=\\sum_{j\\geq0}a^j e_{\\nu j}(U),\\qquad 1\\leq\\nu\\leq s,$$ vanishes at $z_*$ at every selected local datum. Its coefficients satisfy $$\\deg e_{\\nu j}\\leq C(1+j),\\qquad\n     \\mathrm h\\!\\left((e_{\\nu j})_{\\nu,\\,0\\leq j\\leq l}\\right)\n     \\leq Q_3(l,d)\\Psi(h).$$ There are $b_v\\geq0$ with $\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v b_v\\leq Q_4(d)\\Psi(h)$ such that the convergent tails at the target satisfy, for every $l\\geq0$, $$\\label{eq:interpolation-tail}\n     \\left|\\sum_{j\\geq l}a_*^j e_{\\nu j}(U_*)\\right|_v\n     \\leq \\left\\lvert a_*\\right\\rvert_v^l\\exp((1+l)b_v).$$*\n\n4.  *If $W\\subseteq X$ is an irreducible algebraic variety containing $z_*$ on which $a$ is nonconstant, then on every normalization branch above the generic point of every component of $W\\cap V(a)$ the expressions $E_1,\\ldots,E_s$ are not all identically zero. The expressions use their specified ordinary chart germs.*\n\n*Then $h\\leq C_1d^{C_2}$, with constants depending only on the fixed data and the polynomials in the hypotheses.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-zero-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed analytic data of [thm:interpolation](#thm:interpolation) there is a polynomial $M(D)$ with the following property. Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of degree at most $D$ on which $a$ is nonconstant. On a branch above the generic point of a component of $V_W(a)$, every allowed expression $E$ which is not identically zero satisfies $$\\label{eq:divisorial-zero-estimate}\n \\operatorname{ord}_{\\mathfrak p}E\n <M(D)\\operatorname{ord}_{\\mathfrak p}a.$$ The bound is independent of the heights of the coefficients of $W$ and of the expression.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-fiber",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of dimension $k\\geq1$ with coordinate $a$ nonconstant, let $f$ be regular on $W$, and let $m\\ge1$ be an integer. Suppose $$\\operatorname{ord}_{\\mathfrak p}f\n <m\\operatorname{ord}_{\\mathfrak p}a$$ on every normalization branch over the generic components of $V_W(a)$. Let $W'$ be the affine graph closure of $q=f/a^m$ over $W\\setminus V(a)$. For every regular function $g$ on $W$, $$\\label{eq:graph-fiber-dimension}\n \\dim V\\bigl(I(W'),a,q+g\\bigr)\\leq k-2.$$ The set is empty when $k=1$; the same convention applies if $V_W(a)$ is empty.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-fiber",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of dimension $k\\geq1$ with coordinate $a$ nonconstant, let $f$ be regular on $W$, and let $m\\ge1$ be an integer. Let $W'$ be the affine graph closure of $q=f/a^m$ over $W\\setminus V(a)$. For every regular function $g$ on $W$, $$\\label{eq:graph-fiber-dimension}\n \\dim V\\bigl(I(W'),a,q+g\\bigr)\\leq k-2.$$ The set is empty when $k=1$; the same convention applies if $V_W(a)$ is empty.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of dimension $k\\geq1$ with coordinate $a$ nonconstant, let $f$ be regular on $W$, and let $m\\ge1$ be an integer.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W'\\subset\\mathbb A^M$ be irreducible of dimension $k\\geq1$, with coordinates including $a,U,q$. Let $\\mathfrak b=(G_1,\\ldots,G_t)$ have controlled generators and $V(\\mathfrak b)=W'$. Suppose $$\\dim V(\\mathfrak b,a,F_0)\\leq k-2,\n \\qquad F_0=q+g_0(U),$$ and put $F_n=F_0+\\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a^j g_j(U)$, where $m\\ge1$ is an integer and $\\deg g_j\\leq C(m+j+1)$. Here $M,C$ are fixed and all other complexities, including $m$, are polynomially bounded in $d$; the coefficient heights through index $n$ are bounded by $Q(n,d)\\Psi(h)$.*\n\n\n*For any prescribed positive integer $r$ polynomially bounded in $d$, there are positive integers $L$ and $n=rL$, both polynomially bounded in $d$, and a polynomial $P$ such that $$\\label{eq:layer-membership}\n \\deg P\\leq L,\\quad P\\notin I(W'),\\quad\n P=\\sum_{\\lambda=1}^t A_\\lambda G_\\lambda+A F_n+a^n B.$$ The degrees of all the coefficients in this representation are polynomial in $d$, and their joint affine height, including that of $P$, is at most $Q'(d)\\Psi(h)$. The polynomial $Q'$ may depend on the prescribed polynomial bound for $r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W'\\subset\\mathbb A^M$ be irreducible of dimension $k\\geq1$, with coordinates including $a,U,q$. Here $M,C$ are fixed and all other complexities, including $m$, are polynomially bounded in $d$; the coefficient heights through index $n$ are bounded by $Q(n,d)\\Psi(h)$.*\n\n\n*For any prescribed positive integer $r$ polynomially bounded in $d$, there are positive integers $L$ and $n=rL$, both polynomially bounded in $d$, and a polynomial $P$ such that $$\\label{eq:layer-membership}\n \\deg P\\leq L,\\quad P\\notin I(W'),\\quad\n P=\\sum_{\\lambda=1}^t A_\\lambda G_\\lambda+A F_n+a^n B.$$ The degrees of all the coefficients in this representation are polynomial in $d$, and their joint affine height, including that of $P$, is at most $Q'(d)\\Psi(h)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-small-vectors",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. For every target $t$, an extension $L/K(t)$ of degree at most $C$ defines all geometric endomorphisms of $A_t$. There are two independent endomorphisms $u_1,u_2\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_L(A_t)$ which are symmetric and trace free, span $E_t$, and satisfy $$\\label{eq:rosati-size}\n q_{\\lambda_t}(u_j)\\le C(dh)^c\\qquad(j=1,2).$$ The corresponding labeled vectors are denoted $P=(P_1,P_2)$ in de Rham coordinates. Conjugating a labeled target always means conjugating these endomorphisms as well. This pair need not generate the saturated integral cohomology lattice of $E_t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-small-vectors",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. For every target $t$, an extension $L/K(t)$ of degree at most $C$ defines all geometric endomorphisms of $A_t$. There are two independent endomorphisms $u_1,u_2\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_L(A_t)$ which are symmetric and trace free, span $E_t$, and satisfy $$\\label{eq:rosati-size}\n q_{\\lambda_t}(u_j)\\le C(dh)^c\\qquad(j=1,2).$$ The corresponding labeled vectors are denoted $P=(P_1,P_2)$ in de Rham coordinates. Conjugating a labeled target always means conjugating these endomorphisms as well. This pair need not generate the saturated integral cohomology lattice of $E_t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. For every target $t$, an extension $L/K(t)$ of degree at most $C$ defines all geometric endomorphisms of $A_t$. Conjugating a labeled target always means conjugating these endomorphisms as well. This pair need not generate the saturated integral cohomology lattice of $E_t$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-dr-size",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the vectors of [prop:small-vectors](#prop:small-vectors). Their affine coordinates in the fixed frame satisfy $$\\label{eq:dr-global}\n \\mathrm h\\!\\left(z(t),a,P\\right)\\le C(h+\\log d).$$ There are center neighborhoods at every place, with a single integral description outside a fixed finite set of rational primes, such that for any selected weighted set $\\mathcal V$ on the actual center branches, $$\\label{eq:dr-local}\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v\n       \\log\\max(1,\\left\\lVert z(t),P\\right\\rVert_v)\n       \\le C(1+\\log h+\\log d).$$ These statements hold for the finitely many conjugate fixed charts. They also hold after a bounded denominator and a polarized isogeny of any one fixed multiplier are applied to the vectors.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-good-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "At a finite place, suppose a point $t$ is on a sufficiently small actual center branch. After coefficient extension the two polarized abelian schemes have a common special fiber $\\overline A$. More precisely, over a finite local extension $F$ with residue field $k$, put $F_0=W(k)[1/p]$ and $D=H^1_{\\mathrm{cris}}(\\overline A/W(k))[1/p]$. There are rational comparison isomorphisms $$c_u:H^1_{\\mathrm{dR}}(A_u/F)\\longrightarrow D\\otimes_{F_0}F,\n \\qquad u=t_0,t,$$ whose transition $c_{t_0}^{-1}c_t$ is horizontal transport. They are compatible with morphisms of either lifted fiber and their specializations, including morphisms which do not extend over the base curve. Ramification of the coefficient field is allowed.*\n\n\n*In particular, if $\\beta_i$ and $\\alpha_j$ are the specializations of the centered and target endomorphisms, then $$\\label{eq:cross-trace}\n \\left\\langle B_i,Y(a)P_j\\right\\rangle=\\tfrac14\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\beta_i\\alpha_j)\\in\\mathbb Q\n       \\qquad(1\\le i,j\\le2).$$ The cross pairings belong to $\\tfrac14\\mathbb Z$, and their ordinary absolute values are at most $C(dh)^c$. At archimedean places the same assertions hold with integral Betti transport in place of specialization.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-good-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "At a finite place, suppose a point $t$ is on a sufficiently small actual center branch. They are compatible with morphisms of either lifted fiber and their specializations, including morphisms which do not extend over the base curve. Ramification of the coefficient field is allowed.*\n\n\n*In particular, if $\\beta_i$ and $\\alpha_j$ are the specializations of the centered and target endomorphisms, then $$\\label{eq:cross-trace}\n \\left\\langle B_i,Y(a)P_j\\right\\rangle=\\tfrac14\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\beta_i\\alpha_j)\\in\\mathbb Q\n       \\qquad(1\\le i,j\\le2).$$ The cross pairings belong to $\\tfrac14\\mathbb Z$, and their ordinary absolute values are at most $C(dh)^c$. At archimedean places the same assertions hold with integral Betti transport in place of specialization.",

At archimedean places the same assertions hold with integral Betti transport in place of specialization.",
      "explanation": "Near the center at a finite place the two fibres share a special fibre, and horizontal transport is realised by crystalline-de Rham comparison. Cross pairings of centered and target endomorphisms are then rational, lie in a fixed lattice and are polynomially bounded.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-proximity",
        "exact_quote": "Use the fixed center disks of [prop:dr-size](#prop:dr-size), [prop:good-comparison](#prop:good-comparison), and at every remaining prime impose <a href=\"#eq:factorial-disk\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:factorial-disk\">[eq:factorial-disk]</a>. Let $\\mathcal V_0(t)$ be all weighted embeddings at which $t$ belongs to these actual branches. Then $$\\label{eq:proximity-sum}\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V_0(t)}w_v\\log(1/|a|_v)\n       \\ge c h-C\\bigl(1+\\log^2(dh)\\bigr).$$ In particular the sum is at least $c h/2$ if $h\\ge Q_0(d)$ for a fixed polynomial $Q_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-proximity",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-common-relations",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $h\\ge Q_0(d)$, increasing $Q_0$ if necessary. The proximity embeddings can be partitioned into at most $Q_1(d)$ groups, and one group $\\mathcal V$ has $$\\label{eq:group-smallness}\n S_{\\mathrm{small}}=\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v\\log(1/|a|_v)\n       \\ge h/Q_2(d).$$ On each group there is one fixed list of analytic equations, evaluated at one algebraic tuple $(a,U)$, of one of the following forms: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\langle B,Y(a)P\\right\\rangle&=T,\n       &&\\text{with }T\\in\\mathrm{M}_2(\\mathbb Q),\n       \\label{eq:constant-relations}\\\\\n \\left\\langle B,Y(a)P\\right\\rangle&=\\left\\langle B',Y'(aR)P'\\right\\rangle,\n       &&R=b/a,\\quad b=x'(t'),\n       \\label{eq:paired-relations}\n\\end{aligned}$$ together with the actual branch equations $$\\label{eq:chart-relations}\n z-z(a)=0,\n \\qquad z'-z'(aR)=0\n \\quad\\text{when a second chart is present}.$$ The primed data are one simultaneous field conjugate of the complete labeled unprimed data. In <a href=\"#eq:paired-relations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:paired-relations\">[eq:paired-relations]</a> all places are finite and $|R|_v=1$ on the group. The Frobenius of the common reduction transports each of the two labeled pairs to its primed pair.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*The tuple has degree at most $Q_3(d)$ over $K$, and $$\\label{eq:common-size}\n \\mathrm h\\!\\left(a,U\\right)\\le Q_3(d)h,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v\\log\\max(1,\\left\\lVert U\\right\\rVert_v)\n       \\le Q_3(d)(1+\\log h).$$ Writing the common list as $E(a,U)=\\sum_{j\\ge0}a^j e_j(U)$, it has fixed length and $$\\label{eq:coefficient-size}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\deg e_j&\\le C(1+j) &&(j\\ge0),\\\\\n \\mathrm h\\!\\left(\\text{affine coefficient tuple of }(e_0,\\ldots,e_l)\\right)\n       &\\le Q_4(l,d)(1+\\log h) &&(l\\ge0).\n \\end{aligned}$$ There are $b_v\\ge0$ with $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\|\\sum_{j\\ge l}a^j e_j(U)\\right\\|_v\n       &\\le |a|_v^l\\exp\\bigl((1+l)b_v\\bigr)\n             &&(l\\ge0),\\label{eq:arithmetic-tail}\\\\\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v b_v\n       &\\le Q_5(d)(1+\\log^2 h).\n             \\label{eq:tail-sum}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The series are formed from fixed ordinary-point algebraic connection systems in $a$ and $aR$, and fixed algebraic chart series, by polynomial operations of fixed degree.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-common-relations",

In <a href=\"#eq:paired-relations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:paired-relations\">[eq:paired-relations]</a> all places are finite and $|R|_v=1$ on the group. The Frobenius of the common reduction transports each of the two labeled pairs to its primed pair.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*The tuple has degree at most $Q_3(d)$ over $K$, and $$\\label{eq:common-size}\n \\mathrm h\\!\\left(a,U\\right)\\le Q_3(d)h,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v\\log\\max(1,\\left\\lVert U\\right\\rVert_v)\n       \\le Q_3(d)(1+\\log h).$$ Writing the common list as $E(a,U)=\\sum_{j\\ge0}a^j e_j(U)$, it has fixed length and $$\\label{eq:coefficient-size}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\deg e_j&\\le C(1+j) &&(j\\ge0),\\\\\n \\mathrm h\\!\\left(\\text{affine coefficient tuple of }(e_0,\\ldots,e_l)\\right)\n       &\\le Q_4(l,d)(1+\\log h) &&(l\\ge0).\n \\end{aligned}$$ There are $b_v\\ge0$ with $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\|\\sum_{j\\ge l}a^j e_j(U)\\right\\|_v\n       &\\le |a|_v^l\\exp\\bigl((1+l)b_v\\bigr)\n             &&(l\\ge0),\\label{eq:arithmetic-tail}\\\\\n \\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}w_v b_v\n       &\\le Q_5(d)(1+\\log^2 h).\n             \\label{eq:tail-sum}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The series are formed from fixed ordinary-point algebraic connection systems in $a$ and $aR$, and fixed algebraic chart series, by polynomial operations of fixed degree.",

      "explanation": "Frobenius eliminates the rational trace matrix. A polynomial number of groups of places is formed, and one group carries a common list of analytic equations with controlled heights and tail bounds.",

      "id": "claim-common-relations",
      "kind": "construction",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-field-incidence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a finite field of odd characteristic, let $(V,q)$ be a nondegenerate quadratic space of dimension five, let $A\\subset V$ be a nondegenerate $2$-plane, and let $T\\subset V$ be any $2$-plane. There is an element $$u\\in\\operatorname{im}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(V)(k)\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(V)(k)\\bigr)$$ such that $A\\cap uT=0$ and $A+uT$ is a degenerate $4$-plane, with radical of dimension one. The conclusion includes restricted quadratic forms on $T$ of ranks zero, one, and two.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-field-incidence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a finite field of odd characteristic, let $(V,q)$ be a nondegenerate quadratic space of dimension five, let $A\\subset V$ be a nondegenerate $2$-plane, and let $T\\subset V$ be any $2$-plane. There is an element $$u\\in\\operatorname{im}\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(V)(k)\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(V)(k)\\bigr)$$ such that $A\\cap uT=0$ and $A+uT$ is a degenerate $4$-plane, with radical of dimension one. The conclusion includes restricted quadratic forms on $T$ of ranks zero, one, and two.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $k$ be a finite field of odd characteristic, let $(V,q)$ be a nondegenerate quadratic space of dimension five, let $A\\subset V$ be a nondegenerate $2$-plane, and let $T\\subset V$ be any $2$-plane. The conclusion includes restricted quadratic forms on $T$ of ranks zero, one, and two.",
      "explanation": "Over a finite field of odd characteristic, an element lifting to the spin group moves any plane to meet a given nondegenerate plane in a degenerate four-space with one-dimensional radical.",

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-field-incidence"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-marking",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed odd prime $\\ell$ and a fixed geometrically connected finite étale cover $S\\to S_*$ parameterizing symplectic markings at level $\\ell$, with a fixed lift $t_0$ of $t_{0,*}$, such that every quaternionic-division point of $S_*$ outside a finite set has a lift $t\\in S$ satisfying the following condition. In the common quadratic space supplied by the markings, $$\\label{eq:marked-incidence}\n \\dim\\bigl(\\overline L_{t_0}+\\overline L_t\\bigr)=4,\n \\qquad\n q|_{\\overline L_{t_0}+\\overline L_t}\\text{ is degenerate}.$$ The prime $\\ell$ is prime to every $m\\in\\mathcal M$. At every prime $p\\ne\\ell$ of good reduction for these fixed models where $t$ and $t_0$ have the same marked reduction, the corresponding two saturated $\\mathbb Z_\\ell$-lattices have sum $M$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:primitive-four-lattice}\n M=\\Lambda_\\ell\\cap(M\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z_\\ell}\\mathbb Q_\\ell),\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M=4,\n \\qquad M/\\ell M\\text{ is degenerate}.$$ These assertions are preserved by simultaneous field conjugation.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.3"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hodge generic curve** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A curve is *Hodge generic* if it is not contained in a proper special subvariety, including a Hecke translate of one.
- **QM point** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Throughout the paper, a *QM point* means a point whose full geometric rational endomorphism algebra is an indefinite quaternion division algebra over $\mathbb Q$.
- **Ordinary point of a differential system** ([source](#pm-h-4)): In this terminology, “ordinary” means that the differential system has no pole at the center; it imposes no ordinary-reduction condition on the abelian surface.
- **Rational coordinate height** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The rational coordinate height used in point counting is multiplicative and will be denoted $H_{\mathrm{rat}}$.
- **Rosati adjoint notation** ([source](#eq:quadratic-system)): The adjoint of an operator for $\psi$ is denoted by $T^\dagger$.
- **Weights at infinite places** ([source](#pm-h-12)): At the infinite places the absolute value is the usual, unsquared one; thus a complex place has weight $2/[F:\mathbb Q]$.

| q-height-vs-degree | height of a quaternionic point | ≤ | $C_1\max\{2,[K(s):K]\}^{C_2}$ | Constants independent of the quaternion algebra and endomorphism order |
| q-period-quadric-dim | complex dimension of the period quadric | = | $3$ | fixed |
| q-rosati-size | Rosati form of the small endomorphisms | ≤ | $C(dh)^c$ | d degree, h height |
| q-dr-global-height | global height of de Rham coordinates | ≤ | $C(h+\log d)$ | target point |
| q-proximity-sum | weighted proximity sum of the parameter | ≥ | $c h-C\bigl(1+\log^2(dh)\bigr)$ | after the factorial cutoff |
| q-group-smallness | smallness sum of one group of places | ≥ | $h/Q_2(d)$ | large height |
| q-interpolation-smallness | interpolation smallness hypothesis | ≥ | $\frac{h}{Q_1(d)}$ | fixed polynomial |
| q-interpolation-conclusion | height from interpolation | ≤ | $C_1d^{C_2}$ | constants depend on fixed data |
| q-graph-fibre-dim | dimension of the constrained graph fibre | ≤ | $k-2$ | order inequality on all branches |
| q-flat-height | rational height of flat coordinates | ≤ | $c\bigl(d(t)h(t)\bigr)^M$ | each chart containing the point |
| q-counting-hypothesis | height hypothesis of the counting proposition | ≤ | $A d(t)^\kappa$ | fixed A and kappa |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-finite-covers** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-covers): Fine-level moduli, normalization and descent of heights and degrees -> smooth cover with abelian scheme
- **s-qm-plane** [claim](#stmt-lem-qm-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-qm-plane): Explicit operator matrices and Rosati positivity -> quadratic system and positive plane
- **s-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-prop-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-monodromy): Normality of monodromy, Goursat lemma, adjoint group automorphisms -> full single and product monodromy up to isogeny
- **s-finite-correspondences** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-correspondences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-correspondences): Discrete stabilizer, finite area, finite index of integral stabilizer -> finite list of similitudes and multipliers
- **s-zero-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-zero-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-zero-estimate): Slicing to a curve, Riemann–Hurwitz, differential row-space argument -> order bound polynomial in degree
- **s-graph-fiber** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-fiber) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-fiber): Normalization and discrete valuation argument -> dimension drop for the graph fibre
- **s-layer-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-interpolation): Gröbner layer division and linear algebra count of conditions -> auxiliary polynomial of controlled degree and height
- **s-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-thm-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-interpolation-2): Descent through subvarieties using the product formula -> polynomial height bound
- **s-potential-good** [claim](#stmt-lem-potential-good) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-potential-good): Semistable reduction and module dimension over a division algebra -> potentially good reduction
- **s-small-vectors** [claim](#stmt-prop-small-vectors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-small-vectors): Endomorphism estimate, theta height comparison, integral projection -> two small labeled endomorphisms
- **s-dr-size** [claim](#stmt-prop-dr-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dr-size): Denominator ideals, product formula, Schmid norm estimates, center disks -> global and local coordinate bounds
- **s-good-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-good-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-good-comparison): Crystalline-de Rham comparison on an enlargement of the special point -> cross trace relations
- **s-proximity** [claim](#stmt-lem-proximity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-proximity): Height of the center divisor and prime-support loss estimate -> weighted smallness lower bound
- **s-common-relations** [claim](#stmt-prop-common-relations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-common-relations): Grouping by embeddings, Frobenius elimination of the trace, coefficient recursion -> common equations with controlled heights and tails
- **s-finite-field-incidence** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-field-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-field-incidence): Witt extension and spinor norm correction -> spin-liftable element giving degenerate four-space
- **s-fixed-isogeny-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-isogeny-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-isogeny-reduction): Spreading out finite schemes of kernels and polarized isomorphisms -> finite list of lifting isogenies
- **s-marking** [claim](#stmt-prop-marking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-marking): Strong approximation and incidence lemma over the residue field -> auxiliary level cover with degenerate marked incidence
- **s-nonidentity** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonidentity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonidentity): Monodromy continuation, Burnside tensors, Frobenius quasi-isogeny sign exclusion -> nonidentity on all branches
- **s-height** [claim](#stmt-thm-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-height): Apply interpolation to the common relations and descend from selected lifts -> polynomial height bound in the degree
- **s-flat-coordinate-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-flat-coordinate-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flat-coordinate-bound): Nilpotent orbit and Schmid norm estimates on sectors, dual norm inequality -> bound on integral flat coordinates
- **s-counting-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-prop-counting-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-counting-finiteness): Definable path counting, dimension of the incidence variety, Zariski density of the orbit -> finiteness under a polynomial height-degree bound
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-counting-finiteness-2): Contradiction from an infinite locus using the height bound and counting finiteness -> finiteness of the quaternionic locus

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-qm-finiteness**: On a reduced irreducible closed Hodge-generic algebraic curve in the Siegel threefold over the algebraic closure of the rationals, the set of points whose full geometric rational endomorphism algebra is an indefinite quaternion division algebra is finite.
- **inv-degree-height-bound**: For quaternionic-division points on the normalized curve, the height is bounded by a fixed power of the field degree over the base field, with constants independent of the quaternion algebra, its discriminant and the endomorphism order.
- **inv-counting-finiteness**: Under a height bound that is polynomial in the field degree, only finitely many QM points exist on the fixed cover curve.
- **inv-full-monodromy**: The connected algebraic monodromy is the full symplectic group on the weight-one system and the full special orthogonal group on the rank-five system.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-point-isolation**: Use the height bound as an effective procedure to enumerate or locate the quaternionic points. -> The constants in the bound come from a non-constructive argument and are not made explicit, so no search radius or termination criterion follows.
- **anti-non-generic-curve**: Apply the finiteness statement to a curve lying inside a special subvariety. -> The hypothesis of Hodge genericity fails, so finiteness is not asserted.
- **anti-split-algebra**: Apply the result to surfaces whose algebra is the split matrix algebra or merely contains a quaternion embedding. -> The condition is equality of the full geometric endomorphism algebra with a division algebra, so such points are outside the statement.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves finiteness and a height-degree bound by non-constructive means (auxiliary-function descent, o-minimal path counting, Northcott); the constants depend on the curve and are not computed, and no finite procedure with explicit steps is stated that outputs the points or the constants.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves finiteness of a locus and a polynomial height-versus-degree bound. It states no procedure with explicit steps that a program could run, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are moduli of abelian surfaces, period quadrics and Galois orbits. They carry no state, dynamics or geometry that a simulation could use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or interface is described. The finiteness statement could at most serve as a specification fact for a future atypical-intersection engine, and the paper gives no such tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The height bound is proved by auxiliary-function and counting arguments with constants fixed only by existence. The paper gives no algorithm and no effective constants for isolating the points. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation, index or container is introduced. Gröbner bases and Chow forms appear only as proof tools in fixed dimension, not as a structure with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no computation, dataflow or arithmetic kernel in the result to map onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or primitive is involved. The statement concerns finiteness of special points on a curve in a Siegel moduli variety. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no data, model, loss or learning problem. Its estimates are Diophantine and have no statistical content. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation problem is studied. The descent through subvarieties is a proof device, not a minimisation procedure. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Galois orbits and local places are algebraic objects. No communication, consensus or replicated state appears. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only reuse is inside arithmetic geometry, as an input to a wider unlikely-intersection argument for curves in the Siegel threefold. This is research mathematics, not an engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-hodge-generic** (hypothesis): The finiteness theorem needs the curve to be reduced, irreducible, closed and Hodge generic. A curve inside a proper special subvariety, including a Hecke translate of one, is outside the statement. Blocked use: ['Applying finiteness to a curve lying in a proper special subvariety', 'Assuming genericity without checking containment in Hecke translates of special subvarieties']
- **limit-full-endomorphism-algebra** (hypothesis): The locus counted is defined by equality of the full geometric rational endomorphism algebra with an indefinite quaternion division algebra. Points with the split algebra, or with only a quaternion embedding into a larger algebra, are not covered. Blocked use: ['Counting points with split endomorphism algebra under this theorem', 'Replacing equality of the algebra by existence of a quaternion embedding']
- **limit-ineffective-constants** (nonconstructive): The height bound is stated with constants that may depend on the curve, and no procedure is given for computing them or for locating the points. Blocked use: ['Using the bound as an explicit search radius or termination criterion', 'Presenting the result as an algorithm that enumerates quaternionic points']
- **limit-fixed-data-dependence** (formal_scope): Constants and polynomial exponents may depend on fixed curves, covers, centers, frames and connections, but never on the varying quaternion algebra or target point. A polynomial bound refers to a fixed number of variables and fixed connection ranks. Blocked use: ['Reusing the constants for a different curve or a different cover', 'Applying the polynomial estimates with a growing number of variables or ranks']
- **limit-interpolation-nonidentity** (hypothesis): The interpolation theorem needs, on every normalization branch above the generic point of each component of the intersection with the zero locus of the parameter, that the common expressions are not all identically zero. This is verified only on the varieties met in the descent. Blocked use: ['Invoking interpolation when a relation could vanish identically on some branch', 'Applying it to equation lists whose nonidentity has not been shown']
- **limit-large-height-threshold** (edge_case): The proximity and common-relation estimates hold only when the height exceeds a fixed polynomial in the degree. Below that threshold no smallness is asserted. Blocked use: ['Applying the smallness lower bound to points of small height']
- **limit-dimension-count** (counterexample_scope): The graph-fibre dimension drop requires the extra equation. The graph alone can have a boundary fibre of full dimension, as the example with the plane shows. Blocked use: ['Claiming a dimension drop for the graph boundary without imposing the extra equation']
- **limit-counting-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The counting proposition concludes finiteness only for QM points satisfying a height bound polynomial in the degree, with fixed constants. Blocked use: ['Concluding finiteness for points with no polynomial height-degree bound']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the quaternionic-division component of Zilber–Pink for curves in $`\mathcal A_2`$ over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$. A Hodge-generic algebraic curve contains only finitely many points whose full geometric rational endomorphism algebra is an indefinite quaternion division algebra over ℚ. No boundary or reduction hypothesis is required.
